DESIGN.md

show
## Concept
The player taps to cycle a constantly‑moving dot through a set of colors, timing each change so the dot matches the color of upcoming obstacles and can pass safely. The satisfaction comes from the rhythmic “right‑on‑time” moments when a color shift lets the dot glide through a barrier.

## Player
The avatar is a simple colored dot that travels forward automatically. The player’s only control is a tap (or click) that cycles the dot to the next color in its preset sequence. **Verb:** changeColor (triggered by tap). The dot’s movement is continuous; the player does not direct its position.

## Core loop
1. An obstacle appears ahead, its color shown clearly.  
2. The player watches the dot approach and decides whether the current color will match.  
3. If a mismatch is imminent, the player taps to cycle the dot’s color.  
4. The dot reaches the obstacle; if colors match it passes, otherwise the game ends.  
5. Successful passes add to the score and present the next obstacle, typically within 1–3 seconds.

## Hazards / Obstacles
* **Static color barriers** – stationary blocks that block the path unless the dot’s color matches.  
* **Rotating color arms** – bars that spin around a pivot, each segment painted a different color; the dot must slip through the segment that matches its current hue.  
* **Sweeping color sweeps** – horizontal or vertical bars that move across the screen, colored solidly; timing a color change avoids collision.  
* **Color‑gated doors** – openings that open only for a specific color and close for others, requiring precise tapping.  
* **Multi‑color patterns** – composite obstacles composed of several colored sub‑parts that the player must navigate by switching colors multiple times in quick succession.

## Score
* **Pass** – each obstacle cleared awards points.  
* **Combo** – consecutive passes without a miss grant a multiplier.  
* **Color orb collection** – occasional floating orbs of matching color give bonus points.  
* **Streak milestones** – reaching a set number of cleared obstacles yields a bonus reward.

## Win and lose
* **Lose** – colliding with an obstacle whose color does not match the dot’s current color ends the game, accompanied by a visual “shatter” effect and a sound cue.  
* **Win** – completing a predefined sequence of obstacles (e.g., a level of 30 barriers) or surviving a timed wave triggers a celebratory animation, a final score tally, and a “next level” prompt.

## Feel
The game feels fast‑paced yet rhythmic, with neon‑glow visuals and a synth‑wave soundtrack that pulses with each tap. Colors are vivid and contrast sharply against a dark background, giving a sleek, arcade‑style aesthetic. Sound effects are crisp, melodic blips that reinforce successful color matches and stark buzzes for failures.

## Why it's fun
The thrill of perfectly timing a color change to slip through a looming barrier creates a quick, repeatable “aha!” moment that makes players grin, gasp, and strive for higher combos.

TASKS.md

show
1. HTML scaffold & meta.json — create `index.html` with required header, canvas, restart button, empty script block, and matching `meta.json` containing title, description, and controls.
2. Game state objects — declare top‑level objects for player (position, color, velocity), hazards array, score counter, and game phase/state.
3. Main loop setup — implement `startLoop()` using `requestAnimationFrame`, cancel previous loop, wrap body in try/catch, call `update(dt)` and `render()` each frame, clearing the canvas.
4. Input handling — add `keys` Set with `keydown`/`keyup` listeners, map `R` key and restart button click to a shared `reset()` function.
5. Player color cycling — implement tap/click (or key) handler that cycles the player's color through a preset list and updates the player object.
6. Player movement — apply continuous forward motion scaled by `dt` to the player's position each frame.
7. Hazard structure & spawning — define hazard objects (position, color, speed), implement spawn timer with `SAFE_RADIUS` check to avoid spawning on top of the player within the first 3 seconds.
8. Hazard motion — update each hazard's position each frame using `dt`‑scaled velocity.
9. Collision detection — detect player‑hazard collisions; if colors differ, transition to `gameover` state and stop active gameplay.
10. Scoring system — increment score on successful hazard passes, render the score on the canvas top‑left.
11. Rendering — clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player dot (high‑contrast), and HUD each frame.
12. Game‑over overlay — dim the canvas, display "Game Over", final score, and restart instructions.
13. Observable contract — populate `window.__game` each frame with `state`, `score`, `player` coordinates & visibility, and a one‑sentence `objective`.
14. Audio integration — lazily create an `AudioContext` in a `beep()` helper, wrap calls in try/catch, and trigger sounds on hit, score, and death events.

let audioCtx = null;

// === LOOP =============================================================
function startLoop() {
  // Cancel any existing loop to avoid stacking.
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);

  function tick(now) {
    try {
      // Compute time delta, clamped to avoid huge jumps.
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;

      // Clear the canvas for fresh drawing.
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Run update and render if they exist.
      if (typeof update === "function") update(dt);
      if (typeof render === "function") render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
    // === INPUT =============================================================
    // Input handling: track pressed keys and handle reset.
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (ev) => {
      // Map R/r key to reset, others to keys set.
      if (ev.key === 'r' || ev.key === 'R') {
        // Call reset if defined.
        if (typeof reset === 'function') reset();
        return;
      }
      keys.add(ev.key);
    });
    document.addEventListener('keyup', (ev) => {
      keys.delete(ev.key);
    });
    // Restart button click triggers reset.
    restartBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      if (typeof reset === 'function') reset();
    });

prompt (3,686 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is **game design only**. Do NOT write any HTML or JavaScript code. Do NOT specify pixel coordinates, sprite sizes, or pixels-per-second speeds. The next stage will translate your design into an implementation plan; the stage after that will write the code. Your job here is to describe what the game IS, not how it's built.

Title: Color Switch
Concept: Tap to cycle the dot through several colors; each obstacle is colored, and only obstacles matching the dot's current color block it, so you must time color changes to slip through the gaps.

**You must produce one output file: `DESIGN.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("DESIGN.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the design as inline chat text.** The design must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"DESIGN.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward.

`DESIGN.md` MUST contain these sections (use these exact headers; the validator checks for them):

## Concept
One or two sentences in your own words. What does the player do, and why is it satisfying?

## Player
Describe what the player controls — the avatar, ship, cursor, paddle, whatever it is. Visually, what is it (one sentence)? List its verbs (move, jump, shoot, place, type). Keep it abstract — no coordinates, no speeds.

## Core loop
What happens every 1–3 seconds of normal play. Be specific about the moment-to-moment experience: what's the player reading, what are they choosing between, what makes them lean forward.

## Hazards / Obstacles
What stands in the player's way. Describe each kind by behavior and threat — "fast horizontal sweepers that telegraph their path", "stationary blocks that explode when shot". Concepts and behaviors, not numbers.

## Score
What discrete events increase the score. Examples: "survived a wave", "collected a pickup", "killed an enemy at long range". Avoid score = elapsed time as the only mechanic.

## Win and lose
When does the player win? When do they lose? What happens on screen the moment it ends.

## Feel
The vibe. Pace (frantic / steady / meditative). Aesthetic direction (neon / pastel / monochrome / retro CRT). Sound character (chiptune / arcade beeps / silent). One short paragraph.

## Why it's fun
One sentence. The single moment that would make a player smile, curse, or shout. What is this game's hook?

Keep DESIGN.md focused on player experience. The next phase (tasks) will translate this into an implementation plan with concrete numbers.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `DESIGN.md` as the path. function reset() {
  player.x = canvas.width / 2;
  player.y = canvas.height - 100;
  player.vx = PLAYER_SPEED;
  player.vy = 0;
  player.colorIndex = 0;
  score = 0;
  hazards = [];
  phase = "playing";
}
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;
  // move player forward (upwards)
  player.y -= PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  // keep player within bounds
  if (player.y < 0) { player.y = canvas.height; } // loop for demonstration
}
function render() {
  // background
  ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
  ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
  // hazards (if any)
  ctx.fillStyle = "#ff0000";
  hazards.forEach(h => {
    ctx.fillRect(h.x, h.y, h.w, h.h);
  });
  // player dot
  ctx.fillStyle = PLAYER_COLORS[player.colorIndex];
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, 10, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();

prompt (13,911 chars)
## Reference example: "breakout"

The following is a complete, validator-passing game. **Use it as a structural template** — your output must follow the same section ordering, the same patterns for the loop / restart / observable contract, and the same overall code style. Do NOT reproduce this game; you are building a different game described in the prompt below. The example exists only to anchor structure.

Notice in particular:
- The `// === SECTION ===` divider banners and their fixed order: CONFIG → STATE → RESET → LOOP/UPDATE → RENDER → INPUT → AUDIO → OBSERVABLE CONTRACT → BOOT.
- Plain object literals for game state (no `class`).
- A `keys` Set polled each frame instead of mutating game state from key handlers.
- `if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` at the top of `startLoop()`.
- The animation loop body wrapped in `try/catch`.
- `publishState()` called once per frame at the end of the tick.
- `window.__game` populated honestly (player.visible reflects actual viewport position).
- AudioContext initialized lazily inside `beep()`, wrapped in try/catch.

### Example `meta.json`
```json
{
  "title": "Neon Breaker",
  "description": "Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.",
  "controls": [
    "Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle",
    "Hold Space to launch faster",
    "Press R or click Restart"
  ],
  "tags": ["arcade", "breakout", "bouncing"]
}
```

### Example `index.html`
```html
<!DOCTYPE html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
  <meta charset="utf-8">
  <title>Neon Breaker</title>
  <style>
    body { margin: 0; background: #0a0a0f; color: #e6e6f0; font-family: ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }
    .game-header { padding: 12px 16px; border-bottom: 1px solid #1d1d2a; }
    .game-header h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 18px; color: #7df9ff; }
    .game-header .game-description { margin: 4px 0; color: #a5a5b8; }
    .game-header .game-controls { margin: 4px 0; padding-left: 18px; color: #c8c8d8; }
    main { display: flex; flex-direction: column; align-items: center; padding: 16px; }
    canvas { background: #0f1320; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; }
    .game-restart { margin-top: 10px; padding: 8px 14px; background: #1c2030; color: #7df9ff; border: 1px solid #2a2f44; cursor: pointer; }
    .game-restart:hover { background: #232842; }
  </style>
</head>
<body>
  <header class="game-header">
    <h1>Neon Breaker</h1>
    <p class="game-description">Bounce the ball with the paddle and clear every brick. One ball, no second chances.</p>
    <ul class="game-controls">
      <li>Arrow keys or A/D to move the paddle</li>
      <li>Hold Space to launch faster</li>
      <li>Press R or click Restart</li>
    </ul>
  </header>
  <main>
    <canvas id="game" width="800" height="600"></canvas>
    <button id="restart" class="game-restart" type="button">Restart</button>
  </main>
  <script>
  // === CONFIG =============================================================
  const PADDLE_W = 110;
  const PADDLE_H = 14;
  const PADDLE_Y = 560;
  const PADDLE_SPEED = 700;       // px/sec

  const BALL_R = 7;
  const BALL_BASE_SPEED = 360;    // px/sec at start
  const BALL_MAX_SPEED  = 720;
  const BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT = 6; // px/sec added per brick

  const BRICK_ROWS = 6;
  const BRICK_COLS = 12;
  const BRICK_W = 60;
  const BRICK_H = 20;
  const BRICK_GAP = 4;
  const BRICK_TOP = 60;
  const BRICK_LEFT = (800 - (BRICK_COLS * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP) - BRICK_GAP)) / 2;
  const ROW_COLORS = ["#ff52ae", "#ff8c52", "#ffd23f", "#a3ff52", "#52ffae", "#7df9ff"];

  // === STATE ==============================================================
  const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
  const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
  const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

  let paddle;        // { x } center x
  let ball;          // { x, y, vx, vy, speed }
  let bricks;        // array of { x, y, w, h, color, alive }
  let score;
  let bricksLeft;
  let phase;         // "playing" | "won" | "lost"
  let keys;          // pressed key set
  let highScore = 0;
  let lastTime;
  let loopId = null;
  let audioCtx = null;

  function reset() {
    paddle = { x: 400 };
    ball = { x: 400, y: 480, vx: 0, vy: 0, speed: BALL_BASE_SPEED };
    launchBall();
    bricks = [];
    for (let r = 0; r < BRICK_ROWS; r++) {
      for (let c = 0; c < BRICK_COLS; c++) {
        bricks.push({
          x: BRICK_LEFT + c * (BRICK_W + BRICK_GAP),
          y: BRICK_TOP + r * (BRICK_H + BRICK_GAP),
          w: BRICK_W, h: BRICK_H,
          color: ROW_COLORS[r % ROW_COLORS.length],
          alive: true,
        });
      }
    }
    bricksLeft = bricks.length;
    score = 0;
    phase = "playing";
    keys = new Set();
    lastTime = performance.now();
    publishState();
    startLoop();
  }

  function launchBall() {
    // Always launch upward, mild horizontal lean either side.
    const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + (Math.random() * 0.6 - 0.3);
    ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
    ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
  }

  function startLoop() {
    if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
    function tick(now) {
      try {
        const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
        lastTime = now;
        update(dt);
        render();
        publishState();
      } catch (err) {
        console.error("loop error", err);
      }
      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  // === UPDATE =============================================================
  function update(dt) {
    if (phase !== "playing") return;

    // Paddle.
    const dirX = (keys.has("ArrowRight") || keys.has("d") || keys.has("D") ? 1 : 0)
               - (keys.has("ArrowLeft")  || keys.has("a") || keys.has("A") ? 1 : 0);
    const speedMul = keys.has(" ") ? 1.6 : 1;
    paddle.x += dirX * PADDLE_SPEED * speedMul * dt;
    paddle.x = clamp(paddle.x, PADDLE_W / 2, canvas.width - PADDLE_W / 2);

    // Ball motion.
    ball.x += ball.vx * dt;
    ball.y += ball.vy * dt;

    // Walls.
    if (ball.x < BALL_R) { ball.x = BALL_R; ball.vx = Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.x > canvas.width - BALL_R) { ball.x = canvas.width - BALL_R; ball.vx = -Math.abs(ball.vx); beep(220, 0.04); }
    if (ball.y < BALL_R) { ball.y = BALL_R; ball.vy = Math.abs(ball.vy); beep(220, 0.04); }

    // Floor — lose.
    if (ball.y > canvas.height + BALL_R) {
      phase = "lost";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(110, 0.25);
      return;
    }

    // Paddle.
    const px = paddle.x, py = PADDLE_Y;
    if (ball.vy > 0
        && ball.y + BALL_R >= py
        && ball.y - BALL_R <= py + PADDLE_H
        && ball.x >= px - PADDLE_W / 2 - BALL_R
        && ball.x <= px + PADDLE_W / 2 + BALL_R) {
      // Bounce direction depends on where it hit on the paddle.
      const offset = (ball.x - px) / (PADDLE_W / 2); // -1..1
      const angle = (-Math.PI / 2) + offset * 1.0;   // up-ish, leaning side
      ball.vx = Math.cos(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = Math.sin(angle) * ball.speed;
      ball.y = py - BALL_R - 0.1;
      beep(540, 0.05);
    }

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      if (ball.x + BALL_R < b.x || ball.x - BALL_R > b.x + b.w) continue;
      if (ball.y + BALL_R < b.y || ball.y - BALL_R > b.y + b.h) continue;

      b.alive = false;
      bricksLeft--;
      score += 10;
      ball.speed = Math.min(BALL_MAX_SPEED, ball.speed + BALL_SPEEDUP_PER_HIT);

      // Decide which side was hit by comparing penetrations.
      const overlapX = Math.min(ball.x + BALL_R - b.x, b.x + b.w - (ball.x - BALL_R));
      const overlapY = Math.min(ball.y + BALL_R - b.y, b.y + b.h - (ball.y - BALL_R));
      if (overlapX < overlapY) ball.vx = -ball.vx; else ball.vy = -ball.vy;

      // Renormalize to current speed.
      const m = Math.hypot(ball.vx, ball.vy);
      ball.vx = (ball.vx / m) * ball.speed;
      ball.vy = (ball.vy / m) * ball.speed;
      beep(880, 0.05);
      break;
    }

    if (bricksLeft === 0) {
      phase = "won";
      if (score > highScore) highScore = score;
      beep(990, 0.2);
    }
  }

  // === RENDER =============================================================
  function render() {
    ctx.fillStyle = "#0f1320";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

    // Bricks.
    for (const b of bricks) {
      if (!b.alive) continue;
      ctx.fillStyle = b.color;
      ctx.fillRect(b.x, b.y, b.w, b.h);
    }

    // Paddle.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#7df9ff";
    ctx.fillRect(paddle.x - PADDLE_W / 2, PADDLE_Y, PADDLE_W, PADDLE_H);

    // Ball.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ffffff";
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(ball.x, ball.y, BALL_R, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();

    // HUD.
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.textAlign = "left";
    ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
    ctx.textAlign = "right";
    ctx.fillText(`Bricks ${bricksLeft}  ·  Best ${highScore}`, canvas.width - 12, 22);

    if (phase === "lost" || phase === "won") {
      ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
      ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      ctx.textAlign = "center";
      ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillStyle = phase === "won" ? "#52ffae" : "#ff52ae";
      ctx.fillText(phase === "won" ? "You Win!" : "Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
      ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText(`Final score ${score}`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
      ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
      ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
      ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
    }
  }

  // === INPUT ==============================================================
  document.addEventListener("keydown", (ev) => {
    if (ev.key === "r" || ev.key === "R") { reset(); return; }
    keys.add(ev.key);
  });
  document.addEventListener("keyup", (ev) => { keys.delete(ev.key); });
  restartBtn.addEventListener("click", reset);

  // === AUDIO (lazy) =======================================================
  function beep(freq, durSec) {
    try {
      if (!audioCtx) audioCtx = new (window.AudioContext || window.webkitAudioContext)();
      const osc = audioCtx.createOscillator();
      const gain = audioCtx.createGain();
      osc.type = "square";
      osc.frequency.value = freq;
      gain.gain.setValueAtTime(0.05, audioCtx.currentTime);
      gain.gain.exponentialRampToValueAtTime(0.001, audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
      osc.connect(gain); gain.connect(audioCtx.destination);
      osc.start(); osc.stop(audioCtx.currentTime + durSec);
    } catch {}
  }

  // === OBSERVABLE CONTRACT ===============================================
  function publishState() {
    window.__game = {
      state: phase === "playing" ? "playing" : "gameover",
      score,
      player: {
        x: paddle.x,
        y: PADDLE_Y + PADDLE_H / 2,
        visible: paddle.x >= 0 && paddle.x <= canvas.width,
      },
      objective: "Bounce the ball with the paddle to break every brick.",
    };
  }

  // === HELPERS ============================================================
  function clamp(v, lo, hi) { return v < lo ? lo : (v > hi ? hi : v); }

  // === BOOT ===============================================================
  reset();
  </script>
</body>
</html>
```

Title: Color Switch
Concept: Tap to cycle the dot through several colors; each obstacle is colored, and only obstacles matching the dot's current color block it, so you must time color changes to slip through the gaps.

No other values.\n177: - `player.visible` is critical. **If your camera follows the player, the camera math must keep `player.visible` = true at all times.** A camera that lags the player off-screen is the most common failure here.\n178: - `objective` is one short sentence written for a first-time player: \"what am I trying to do?\" — derivable from observing the game alone, not from reading the meta.\n179: \n180: If your game has multiple \"players\" or no clear single player, expose the position of the most player-like entity (the thing the human controls).\n181: \n182: ## What the validator checks\n183: \n184: Your output is checked programmatically. function startLoop() {
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);
  function tick(now) {
    try {
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;
      // clear canvas
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
      // Placeholder update/render
      if (typeof update === 'function') update(dt);
      if (typeof render === 'function') render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error('loop error', err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }
  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}

```js
// === CONFIG =============================================================
... // (none)

// === STATE ==============================================================
...

// === LOOP =============================================================
function startLoop() {
  // Cancel any existing loop to avoid stacking.
  if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);

  function tick(now) {
    try {
      // Compute time delta, clamped to avoid huge jumps.
      const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
      lastTime = now;

      // Clear the canvas for fresh drawing.
      ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

      // Run update and render if they exist.
      if (typeof update === "function") update(dt);
      if (typeof render === "function") render();
    } catch (err) {
      console.error("loop error", err);
    }
    loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
  }

  loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
}
```

Script segment from start:

```
    // === CONFIG =============================================================
    // (none)

    // === STATE ==============================================================
    const canvas = document.getElementById("game");
    const ctx = canvas.getContext("2d");
    const restartBtn = document.getElementById("restart");

    // Player state
    let player = {
      x: canvas.width / 2,
      y: canvas.height - 100,
      vx: 0,
      vy: 0,
      colorIndex: 0,
    };

    // Hazards array
    let hazards = [];

    // Score and phase
    let score = 0;
    let phase = "menu"; // "menu" | "playing" | "gameover"

    // Input set
    let keys = new Set();

    // Loop control
    let loopId = null;
    let lastTime = performance.now();

    // Audio context (lazy)
    let audioCtx = null;

    // === LOOP =============================================================
    function startLoop() {
      // Cancel any existing loop to avoid stacking.
      if (loopId !== null) cancelAnimationFrame(loopId);

      function tick(now) {
        try {
          // Compute time delta, clamped to avoid huge jumps.
          const dt = Math.min((now - lastTime) / 1000, 0.1);
          lastTime = now;

          // Clear the canvas for fresh drawing.
          ctx.clearRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);

          // Run update and render if they exist.
          if (typeof update === "function") update(dt);
          if (typeof render === "function") render();
        } catch (err) {
          console.error("loop error", err);
        }
        loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
      }

      loopId = requestAnimationFrame(tick);
    }
    // === INPUT =============================================================
    // Input handling: track pressed keys and ... etc
```

We need to ensure each comment line starts with `//` and not accidentally causing syntax errors. The code is fine.

Implement spawnHazard() function:

```
function spawnHazard() {
   // Try up to some attempts to find safe spawn.
   const maxAttempts = 10;
   for (let attempt = 0; attempt < maxAttempts; ++attempt) {
      const x = Math.random() * canvas.width;
      const y = -HAZARD_RADIUS; // start above canvas
      const colorIndex = Math.floor(Math.random() * PLAYER_COLORS.length);
      // Safe radius check
      const dx = x - player.x;
      const dy = y - player.y;
      const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
      // Time to reach player's y (if moving downward)
      const speed = HAZARD_SPEED;
      const timeToReach = (player.y - y) / speed;
      if (dist >= SAFE_RADIUS && timeToReach >= 3) {
         hazards.push({x, y, colorIndex, speed});
         break;
      }
   }
}
```

But note: player.y starts near bottom (canvas.height - 100). At start, y = player.y = canvas.height - 100, so spawn at -hazardRadius far above, distance maybe large. So safe.

   // Initialize player position and state

   player.y = canvas.height - 100;

   player.vy = 0;

}

Now add update(dt) function:

```
function update(dt) {
   if (phase !== "playing") return;

   // Player forward motion
   player.y -= PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
   // Keep player within canvas bounds (optional)
   if (player.y < 0) player.y = 0;

   // Update spawn timer
   spawnTimer -= dt;
   if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
       spawnHazard();
       spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
   }

   // Update hazards
   for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
       const h = hazards[i];
       h.y += h.speed * dt;
       // Collision detection
       const dx = h.x - player.x;
       const dy = h.y - player.y;
       const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
       const collisionDist = HAZARD_RADIUS + PLAYER_RADIUS;
       if (dist < collisionDist) {
           if (h.colorIndex !== player.colorIndex) {
               // Colors differ, game over
               phase = "gameover";
               // Optionally beep
           } else {
               // Colors match, safe pass, will be removed later when off-screen and score incremented.
           }
       }

       // Remove hazard if off-screen
       if (h.y - HAZARD_RADIUS > canvas.height) {
           // Passed player successfully; increment score if still playing
           if (phase === "playing") {
               score++;
           }
           hazards.splice(i, 1);
       }
   }

   // Publishing observable state
   publishState();
}
```

But per reference, publishState is called after render, not inside update. We'll keep as after render. However we can call at the end of tick. Since our startLoop currently only invokes update and render; after render we will call publishState. So we need to add a call to publishState after render in the tick. Now implement render():

```
function render() {
   // Draw hazards
   for (const h of hazards) {
       ctx.fillStyle = PLAYER_COLORS[h.colorIndex];
       ctx.beginPath();
       ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, HAZARD_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
       ctx.fill();
   }

   // Draw player
   ctx.fillStyle = PLAYER_COLORS[player.colorIndex];
   ctx.beginPath();
   ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
   ctx.fill();

   // HUD: score
   ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
   ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
   ctx.textAlign = "left";
   ctx.fillText(`Score ${score}`, 12, 22);
}
```

function publishState() {

    };
}

```
 // === UPDATE =============================================================
function update(dt) {
  if (phase !== "playing") return;

  // Player forward motion
  player.y -= PLAYER_SPEED * dt;
  if (player.y < 0) player.y = 0;

  // Spawn hazards
  spawnTimer -= dt;
  if (spawnTimer <= 0) {
    spawnHazard();
    spawnTimer = SPAWN_INTERVAL;
  }

  // Update hazards
  for (let i = hazards.length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {
    const h = hazards[i];
    h.y += h.speed * dt;

    // Collision detection
    const dx = h.x - player.x;
    const dy = h.y - player.y;
    const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
    const collDist = HAZARD_RADIUS + PLAYER_RADIUS;
    if (dist < collDist) {
      if (h.colorIndex !== player.colorIndex) {
        phase = "gameover";
      }
    }

    // Remove off-screen hazards and score if passed
    if (h.y - HAZARD_RADIUS > canvas.height) {
      if (phase === "playing") {
        score++;
      }
      hazards.splice(i, 1);
    }
  }
}

// === RENDER =============================================================
function render() {
  // Draw hazards
  for (const h of hazards) {
    ctx.fillStyle = PLAYER_COLORS[h.colorIndex];
    ctx.beginPath();
    ctx.arc(h.x, h.y, HAZARD_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
    ctx.fill();
  }

  // Draw player
  ctx.fillStyle = PLAYER_COLORS[player.colorIndex];
  ctx.beginPath();
  ctx.arc(player.x, player.y, PLAYER_RADIUS, 0, Math.PI * 2);
  ctx.fill();

  // HUD
  ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
  ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
  ctx.textAlign = "left";
  ctx.fillText(\`Score \${score}\`, 12, 22);

  // Game over overlay
  if (phase === "gameover") {
    ctx.fillStyle = "rgba(10,10,15,0.7)";
    ctx.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height);
    ctx.textAlign = "center";
    ctx.fillStyle = "#ff52ae";
    ctx.font = "44px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillText("Game Over", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 - 20);
    ctx.fillStyle = "#e6e6f0";
    ctx.font = "20px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillText(\`Final score \${score}\`, canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 14);
    ctx.fillStyle = "#a5a5b8";
    ctx.font = "16px ui-sans-serif, system-ui";
    ctx.fillText("Press R or click Restart", canvas.width / 2, canvas.height / 2 + 42);
  }
}

// === INPUT =============================================================
```

prompt (5,151 chars)
Read CONVENTIONS.md and DESIGN.md in your working directory before doing anything else.

Your job in this stage is to break the implementation into a numbered list of small, ordered tasks. The next stage will work through this list one task at a time, in a single LLM session, reloading the page after each task and capturing console errors for you to fix.

Title: Color Switch
Concept: Tap to cycle the dot through several colors; each obstacle is colored, and only obstacles matching the dot's current color block it, so you must time color changes to slip through the gaps.

**You must produce one output file: `TASKS.md` in the working directory.** Use your write/edit tool to create this file on disk. After your turn ends, the validator runs `readFile("TASKS.md")` — if the file does not exist on disk, validation fails and your work is wasted. **Do NOT reply with the task list as inline chat text.** The list must be written to the file.

**Tool calls MUST be emitted as actual function/tool invocations — NOT described or simulated inside your reasoning/analysis text.** Writing tool-shaped JSON like `{"filePath":"TASKS.md","content":"..."}` *inside your chain-of-thought* does NOT invoke any tool. It is just text in the analysis channel that nothing reads or executes. The same goes for `{"pattern":"**/*"}` etc. — those only do something when emitted as a real `tool_use` event from the action channel, never when written into your thinking. Several prior runs have failed by hallucinating tool calls inside reasoning; do not repeat that pattern. If at any point you "describe" a tool call in your thinking instead of invoking it, you must immediately invoke the real tool afterward. The format MUST be a numbered list, one task per line, each line starting with `<number>.`:

```
1. <Task title> — <one-sentence description of what to do>
2. <Task title> — <one-sentence description>
...
```

The next stage parses tasks by matching `^\d+\.` at the start of each line. Don't use bullet sub-lists, don't number sub-tasks (1.1, 1.2). One number, one task, one line.

### What makes a good task

- **Small enough** to complete in a single coding turn (~50–150 lines of edits).
- **Testable**: after this task, what does the page do that it didn't before?
- **Leaves the file runnable**: at the end of the task, no syntax errors, no uncaught exceptions on load. Even if the game isn't playable yet, it must load.
- **Specific**: don't say "implement player". Say "implement player movement: arrow keys set keys.left/right; in update(), apply acceleration in the pressed direction with cap at MAX_SPEED, friction otherwise; clamp x to canvas bounds."
- **Concrete numbers**: this is where pixel sizes, ms intervals, and px/sec speeds live. Pick them. The implement stage will use them verbatim.

### Required coverage (in roughly this order)

Cover at minimum, ordered as below. Add tasks if the design demands more (powerups, level progression, screen wrap, etc):

1. **HTML scaffold + meta.json**: header (h1, description, controls list), canvas, restart button per CONVENTIONS.md. Stub `<script>` block (empty). meta.json matching header text.
2. **Game state objects**: player, hazards, score, phase using plain object literals at the top of the script. Set initial values.
3. **requestAnimationFrame loop**: startLoop() with `cancelAnimationFrame(loopId)` discipline at the top. Body wrapped in try/catch. Calls update(dt) and render(). Don't draw anything yet besides clearing the canvas.
4. **Input**: a `keys` Set populated by document keydown/keyup. R-key restart hook. Restart button click hook. Both call the same reset() function.
5. **Player movement**: dt-based physics tied to the key inputs from task 4. Verify each control listed in meta.json actually changes a value.
6. **Hazards**: data structure, spawn rules with a SAFE_RADIUS so nothing spawns on top of the player at t=0, motion (dt-scaled).
7. **Collision detection**: player vs hazards. On hit, transition phase to "gameover" and stop the active gameplay.
8. **Score**: discrete events that increase score, drawn on canvas at top-left.
9. **Render**: clear canvas, draw background, hazards, player, HUD. Player must be the highest-contrast object on screen.
10. **Game-over overlay**: dim background, "Game Over", final score, "Press R or click Restart".
11. **Observable contract**: populate `window.__game = { state, score, player:{x,y,visible}, objective }` every frame. `state` is "playing" | "gameover". `objective` is a 10–200 char single-sentence description.
12. **Audio**: lazy-init AudioContext inside a beep() helper, wrapped in try/catch. Hook into hit / score / death events.

You may merge two trivial tasks if they truly belong together (e.g. tasks 1+2 if state is just three lines). You may split a complex task in two. Aim for 10–15 tasks total.

**Final check before you stop**: confirm you actually called your write/edit tool with `TASKS.md` as the path. If you only described the task list in chat without invoking the tool, the file will not exist on disk and validation will fail. After the file is written, stop. Do not begin implementation.
